| B705 | High | One unwritable note in a pull batch: it never arrived, and after a restart every note of the batch doubled on the account (H3-SYNC-3) | Fixed, in the hunt integration PR ([#1094](https://github.com/lxorb/orden/pull/1094)) | The throw left `pullDocs` before `core.commit` | `project` catches a refusal (`notWritten`), retries, toast after 3; sync writes `arrived: true`, so `Con.md` lands where the platform can hold it | test/unwritable.test.ts; notes.rs; app killed mid-batch stays open, see status |

| B710 | High | Signed out, a file changed by git or an agent was overwritten by the next key in the open tab, no version (H3-DESKTOP-6) | Fixed, in the hunt integration PR ([#1094](https://github.com/lxorb/orden/pull/1094)) | No watch without an account, and `write_note` writes without looking | `writeOne` always through `across.writing`; workspace/outside.svelte.ts runs the watch with no engine, re-read on focus | Tests "a stale buffer...", "what the watch says...", "the watch of the spaces"; fixed, not run on Tauri |
| B711 | High | A CRLF note open in two windows: a throw left a window unable to hear or save, and all its typing was lost (H3-WINDOWS-1) | Fixed, in the hunt integration PR ([#1094](https://github.com/lxorb/orden/pull/1094)) | `NoteDoc` held raw CRLF over an LF rope, `took` set `quiet` before a call that can throw | `unixLines` wherever words come in; `quietly` in try/finally; a failed merge gives the rope the words, a failed write retries | across.test.ts "a file with CRLF endings"; drive windows-across.py; not run on Tauri, CEF, WebView2 or WKWebView |

### Hunt status

Integration: `claude/hunt-round-1`; the final run is in `findings/hunt-final.md`.
The reviews found many bugs in the fixes, folded above, and older ones with their own rows: B695, B698 to B702, B706, B716 to B724, B739, B740, B747, B757. B741 has no severity in its source. Commits not given for B650.

| Severity | Found | Fixed | Open |
| --- | --: | --: | --: |
| Critical | 2 | 2 | 0 |
| High | 57 | 57 | 0 |
| Medium | 106 | 106 | 0 |
| Low | 44 | 30 | 14 |

No Critical, High or Medium is wholly open. Left in part or as a limit: a typed reply with no window on macOS (B683); plain `<a href=/delete-account>` and a listener on a link's ancestor stay free, and a button that says nothing goes (B713, B717); a secret field shown and gone inside 70 ms between two scans (B702); an app killed in a pull batch can double those notes, up to `PULL_BATCH` (B705); a kill after the crate's write and before the session's persist writes old words back (B737); a fold's first stall in WebKit, cause not found (hunt-known 8); wayland deb's "a click on another tab" until `f645b7fb6` reaches this branch (hunt4-integ).
Needs a decision: H1-TAURI2-17, H1-TAURI2-18, H4-SYNC-L3 (B770, B780); `write_note`'s whole-text replace (B773).
Not run on platform or at all: B607, B620 to B622, B665, B666, B683 to B685; the windows rows of rounds 3 and 4 ran on the web build only.

## Editor core

Burn-down of 2026-11-15 on `claude/project-thread-19rxjr`, from `unstable`: markdown editing and
rendering, undo/redo, paste and drop, embeds, front matter and autosave. Found by reading the code
by area and by a round-trip fuzz of the editor's commands; each bug reproduced with a failing test
first.

| # | Severity | Bug | Status | Root cause | Fix | Verification |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| EC1 | Medium | Numbered list over a bullet list gave `1. - one`, bullet list over a numbered one gave `- 1. one`, and taking numbers off a nested list dropped its indent | Fixed | `toggleOrderedList` inserted at the line start and only knew numbers; `toggleBulletList` only knew bullets | Both replace whichever list marker is there and keep the indent and the task box; numbers count per indent | 4 `commands.test.ts` tests (failed before); the old test that expected `1. - one` corrected; editor suite passed |
| EC2 | Medium | Bold, italic, strikethrough or code with the caret inside a word already marked that way put empty markers in the middle (`**bo****ld**`) instead of taking the mark off | Fixed | `toggleWrap` only unwrapped markers right at the caret or the selection's edges, never the run the caret stands in | A caret inside a `StrongEmphasis`, `Emphasis`, `Strikethrough` or `InlineCode` run of that mark takes the run's markers off (`*`/`_` alike) and keeps the caret in the words | 2 `commands.test.ts` tests (failed before); editor suite 2076 passed |
| EC3 | Medium | Duplicating a selection over a heading or a nested list copied the blocks inside it again, once per level (`# H` + `para` gave `para` three times), and the caret landed off the last copy | Fixed | `blocksIn` returns a heading's section and a list's items as spans of their own, inside the outer span; `duplicateBlocks` copied each one, and took the caret before the earlier inserts moved it | Only the outermost spans are copied; the caret is placed through the composed change | 3 `bulk.test.ts` tests (failed before); editor suite 2080 passed |
| EC4 | Medium | A heading on a quoted line went in front of the quote (`# > Title`), and could not be taken off or changed there | Fixed | `setHeading` and `shiftHeading` read and wrote from the line's start, past no `>` | Both work after the quote markers, as `toggleTaskList` does | 2 `commands.test.ts` tests (failed before) |
| EC5 | Medium | Edit > Paste inside a code block turned the clipboard into markdown: a copied `<pre>` closed the fence early and spilled the rest as prose, tab-separated text became a table | Fixed | `pasteHere` (the menu's paste) had no code check; the keyboard paste falls back to plain text there | `pasteHere` inserts the plain text when any selection is in code | `paste-menu.test.ts` (2 tests, failed before) |
| EC6 | Medium | With smart punctuation on, typing markdown broke it: a table divider `\| --- \|` turned into dashes so the table never formed, `<!-- -->` became `<!– –>`, and `[[Bob's notes]]` linked to a note named with a curly `’` | Fixed | Rules spared only a line of dashes, and the syntax nodes that keep quotes straight do not exist yet while a comment or link is still open | `smartReplacement` substitutes nothing inside an open `[[` or `<!--` or while `<!--` is typed, and spares dashes on a divider row | `typography.test.ts` "markdown being typed" (3 tests failed before) |
| EC7 | Medium | Reformat document pulled a list item, heading or quote with a `\|` in it right under a table into the table as a row | Fixed | `readTable` kept reading while lines held a pipe, past where the parser ends the `Table` node | The table's lines end where its `Table` node ends | `reformat.test.ts` "a block right under a table" (failed before) |
| EC8 | Medium | Picking a block with no name yet from `[[Note#^` / `[[#^` completion could write the link over the words before it (`See [[#^alp` became `[[#^abc123]][[#^alp`) when the name went into the note being typed in | Fixed | `name()` kept the completion's `from`/`to` across the naming `await`; the namer's own ` ^id` above moved the line | The range is carried through what changed meanwhile; if the change touched the link being typed, nothing is written | `complete.test.ts` "a block named while its link is picked" (failed before) |
| EC9 | Medium | The reading view showed a broken picture for an embed whose name has a space, an accent or `&` (`![[Pasted image 2024.png]]` kept in `attachments/`), though the editor showed it | Fixed | `withPictures` handed `notePicture` the src as the HTML attribute writes it (`Pasted%20image…`, `Q&amp;A.png`), and the space lookup compared that with the file's real name | The attribute text is unescaped (`attributeText`) and the link encoding decoded before the lookup; the answer is escaped back into the attribute | `note-images.test.ts` encoded names, `render.test.ts` "a picture the reading view draws" (both failed before); link-index and reading suites 235 passed |
| EC10 | High | An HTML block with an `<img>` in it was drawn and edited as that one picture: `<div><img a><img b></div>` showed only `a`, and resizing it, editing its alt or resetting its size wrote one `<img>` over the whole block, deleting `b`, the wrapper and any words; `<p>See <img> here</p>` hid its words | Fixed | `parseHtmlImage` accepted any text with `<img` in it, and the span it gave was the whole `HTMLBlock` | A block is a picture only when its one `<img>` is all it holds besides tags; edits rewrite that tag alone, as a tag | `image.test.ts` "an HTML block with a picture in it" (2), `widget.test.ts` wrapped alt edit (all failed before); live-preview suite 362 passed |
| EC11 | Medium | Pressing a picture's resize corner and letting go without dragging threw away its width: `![photo\|300](a.png)` or `<img width="320">` jumped back to full size | Fixed | `finish` rewrote the picture at `image.zoom` (100) whenever it had a `width`, drag or no drag, and `withZoom` drops the width | Nothing is written unless the pointer moved | `widget.test.ts` "a corner pressed and let go without a drag" (failed before) |
| EC12 | Medium | Typing a time and pressing Enter turned it into an emoji: `Lunch 12:00` became `Lunch 12💯`, `9:30` a clock; the same in code (`x:int`, `color:red`) | Fixed | `emojiCompletions` opened on any `:` and two letters, after a word or a digit, and in code, and Enter accepts | It opens only on a colon that starts a word, and never in code | new `emoji.test.ts` (2 tests failed before) |
| EC13 | Medium | A link suggestion picked with the caret inside a link already written left the rest behind (`[[Proje\|ct Plan]]` gave `[[Project Plan]]ct Plan]]`), and `[[` in code opened note suggestions that Enter wrote into the code | Fixed | `insert` replaced from `[[` to the caret and took `]]` only when it stood right at the caret; `wikilinkCompletions` had no code check, unlike the slash and tag menus | The rest of the name is replaced too, and a heading or label after it kept; no suggestions in code | 3 `complete.test.ts` tests (failed before); wikilink suite passed |
| EC14 | Medium | Enter in a code block inside a quote or callout dropped the `> `, so the caret and every later code line left the quote and cut the fence off; on a `- ```js` line it left the list item | Fixed | Markup continuation declines inside `FencedCode` and the fallback Enter copies only leading spaces | `continueInCode` carries the quote markers, the item's indent and the code's own indent onto the new line when the fence sits in a quote or list item | `commands.test.ts` "Enter in code inside a quote or a list item" (3 tests); editor suite 2104 passed |
| EC15 | Medium | Enter on a new fence inside a list item wrote the closing fence at column 0, so the block left the item and an unclosed fence swallowed the rest of the note; a quoted fence was not closed at all | Fixed | `closeFence` inserted `\n\n` + the mark with none of the opening line's container, and read fences only from the line start | The opening line's quote and list prefix is carried onto the blank line and the closer, and other lines are read past theirs | `commands.test.ts` "closes a fence inside a list item or a quote inside it" |
| EC16 | High | A code block's language or a picture's alt text typed and left by clicking another tab was written into the note the pane switched to (at the same offset, when the text there matched, e.g. two notes opening with the same block or banner), and lost from its own | Fixed | Both commits run as the widget is torn down by the switch, after a microtask, against `view.state`, which by then is the other note | Both go through `later(view)`, taken when the edit starts: the note it started in gets the edit, through its document if the pane has moved | `torn-down.test.ts` and `widget.test.ts` "…the pane has moved to another note" (both failed before: the alt went into the other note) |
| EC17 | Medium | A note named from a title lost every hyphen: `Q3-planning` in the Save dialog saved `Q3 planning.md`, splitting under `## Follow-up items` made `Follow up items.md` | Fixed | `nameFromTitle`'s character class ended `* -]`, so `-` was swapped for a space with the characters no file system takes | `-` is kept; a title of nothing but dashes, dots and spaces names nothing | `note-name.test.ts` 2 tests (the hyphen one failed before); 1282 tests of its callers passed |
| EC18 | Medium | Pasting a list with a sub-list written straight inside the outer list (`<ul><li>a</li><ul><li>b</li></ul>…`, as Apple Notes and Google Docs write it) came out flat and loose: `- a\n\n- b\n\n- c`; in a numbered list the items after it were numbered one too high | Fixed | Turndown nests a list only when its parent is an `li`; the item numbering counted every child of the list, the stray `ul` too | A list inside a list is indented under the item before it, and items are counted among items only | `from-html.test.ts` "a list written straight inside a list" (failed before); markdown suite 1904 passed |
| EC19 | Medium | A dropped file embedded by its bare name opened another file of that name: drop a new `report.pdf` with one already at the space's top, and `![[report.pdf]]` showed the old one | Fixed | `markdownFor` always wrote the bare name, and a bare name resolves to the shallowest file that has it | When another file in the space has the name, the embed carries the file's path in the space (`![[assets/report.pdf]]`), as Obsidian's "shortest path when possible" does | `images.test.ts` "a dropped file named like another file in the space" (failed before); editor suite passed |
| EC20 | Medium | A dropped or pasted file with a name over 120 characters was saved with its extension cut off, so it no longer opened as what it was and its embed showed as a missing note | Fixed | `safe_name` took the first 120 characters of the whole name | The stem is shortened and the extension (up to 16 characters) kept | `assets.rs` `keeps_the_extension_of_a_name_it_shortens`, run with `caps_how_long_a_name_can_get` in a standalone `rustc --test` of the two functions (2 passed); `rustfmt --check` clean. The crate's own cargo test run was not done here (CEF build) |
| EC21 | Low | Enter at the end of a numbered task (`1. [ ] t`) gave `2. ` with no box, and Enter on an empty one (`1. [ ] `) gave another item instead of ending the list, as bullets do | Fixed | The library's list continuation knows a task box only after bullets | `continueLists` gives the new numbered item a box when the one before had one, and an empty numbered task ends the list | `commands.test.ts` "Enter at the end of a numbered task" (2 tests); editor suite passed |
| EC22 | Low | A link the paste wrote between angle brackets (`[t](<https://x.com/a)b>)`, for an address with unbalanced parentheses) or with escaped brackets could not be Ctrl/Cmd-clicked | Fixed | `hrefOf` read the URL node's text as written, `<…>` and `\(` included, and turned it down | The brackets are taken off and escapes read before the address is judged | `links.test.ts` 2 tests (failed before) |
| EC23 | Low | `[^Note]` with `[^note]: …` had no jump and no hover preview, though markdown matches footnote labels whatever their case | Fixed | Labels were compared with `===` | Labels are compared case- and space-folded, as GFM does | `footnotes.test.ts` "finds its definition whatever case" (failed before) |
| EC24 | Low | After moving a table column or row past the one the caret was in, the caret stayed at its old index, now in the moved cell instead of its own | Fixed | `movedColumn`/`movedRow` remapped only the moved index, not the one it swapped with | The caret's index is carried through the move | `edits.test.ts` "the caret after a move" (2 tests failed before); table suite 107 passed |
| EC25 | Low | An interactive HTML block whose text shows an entity (`Type &lt;b&gt; here`) rendered a real `<b>` in its frame instead of the words `<b>` | Fixed | The block's `srcdoc` was written with `escape`, which spares written entities, so the frame decoded them a second time | `srcdoc` is written with `escapeAll`, so the frame gets the block exactly as written | `html-block.test.ts` "keeps the entities the block writes" (fails before); markdown `html-block`/`render` tests pass |
| EC26 | Low | Changing the words of a sized markdown picture (`![a cat\|300](a.png)`) rewrote it as an `<img … width="300">` tag, and `\|300x200` lost its height | Fixed | `imageMarkup` wrote any picture with a width as a tag, and the parsed picture kept only the width | A markdown picture keeps its size suffix exactly as written (escaped bar in tables included) and stays markdown | `image.test.ts` "keeps its size, and stays markdown, when its words change" (fails before) |
| EC27 | Medium | Regression from EC22: an autolink `<https://a.test/\[1\]>` lost its backslashes on click, though CommonMark keeps them there | Fixed | EC22 unescaped every address in `hrefOf`, autolinks included | Unwrapping and unescaping moved to `linkDestination`, used only for `[text](destination)` links | `decorate.test.ts` "an autolink keeps its backslashes" (failed with EC22), `links.test.ts` EC22 tests still pass |
| EC28 | Medium | Regression from EC15: Enter after a closing fence could open a new block that swallowed the rest of the note, when an earlier fence sat on a list-marker line or was left open by the end of a quote | Fixed | `closeFence` counted fence lines through the whole note with container prefixes stripped, so a fence closed by indentation or by its quote ending flipped the count | `closeFence` asks the syntax tree whether the fence on this line opens a block with no closing mark | `commands.test.ts` "leaves a closing fence alone after a block in a nested list item" and "… a quote ended" (fail before); all 20 fence tests pass |
| EC29 | Medium | Turning a nested bullet list into a numbered one flattened it: `- a` / `  - b` became `1. a` / `  1. b`, which markdown reads as two siblings | Fixed | The new marker is wider than `- `, but children kept their old indent, now below the parent's content column | Each item's change of marker width is carried to the lines nested under it | `commands.test.ts` "keeps a child under its parent when the markers change width" (fails before) |
| EC30 | Medium | The bullet and numbered list toggles on a quoted line wrote the marker before the `>` (`- > one`), taking the line out of the quote; same cause as EC4 in sibling commands | Fixed | `toggleLinePrefix`/`toggleOrderedList` worked on the raw line, quote marks included | Both toggles share `toggleList`, which works after the quote marks, as the task toggle already did | `commands.test.ts` "puts the list inside a quote, and takes it off again" (fails before) |
| EC31 | Low | Pressing a picture's resize corner with a slight vertical wobble (or a trackpad tap) could still snap a sized picture to a zoom | Fixed | EC11 counted any pointer move as a drag, even one with no horizontal travel | A drag starts only once the pointer moves sideways | `widget.test.ts` "a corner pressed and let go without a drag…" extended with a vertical move (fails before) |
| EC32 | Low | Regression from EC10: an HTML block holding only a picture whose alt has a `>` (`alt="a > b"`), or a picture beside an `&nbsp;`, was no longer drawn as a picture | Fixed | The tag stripper ignored quotes, and `&nbsp;` counted as words | Tags are stripped quote-aware and non-breaking spaces count as space | `image.test.ts` "is its one picture when its words hold a > or it has a space beside it" (fails before) |
| EC33 | Low | Picking a suggestion inside `[[Proj\|ect\\|shown]]` (a link in a table cell) ate the backslash, so the bare `\|` split the cell | Fixed | The rest of the name ran up to `\|` and took the `\` with it | The name stops before an escaped bar, and an escaped bar counts as the start of a label | `complete.test.ts` "and keeps a heading or a label written after the name" extended (fails before) |
| EC34 | Low | Regression from EC19: a dropped file whose space path holds `#`, `\|`, `[`, `]` or `^` (attachment folder named after a note like `C# tips`) was embedded as `![[C# tips/report.pdf]]`, which reads as a heading link | Fixed | The path was written into the wikilink as is, and a wikilink target cannot hold those marks | Such a path falls back to the file's name, as before EC19 | `images.test.ts` "by its name when its path holds a mark a link reads as its end" (fails before) |
| EC35 | Medium | In Read-only mode, ArrowDown/ArrowUp got stuck for good at any drawn block two or more lines tall (table, `$$` math, diagram, embed, HTML card) | Fixed | `edgeBeside` only looked at the line next to the caret; from a block's edge that is the block's own next line, so it returned the same edge again (edit mode hid this, since the block opens at its edge) | When the caret sits on a drawn block's near edge, the step goes to its far edge, and the press after that is the ordinary one | `stepping.test.ts` "a press across a block that never opens, as in read-only mode" (3 fail before) |
| EC36 | Medium | Find opened on a selection holding backslashes matched the wrong text (`"\n"` found nothing, `C:\\Users` searched `C:\Users`), a regex-mode seed `a.b(c)` matched `axbc` (and `C:\Users` threw), and a typed plain replacement `C:\new\table` inserted a newline and a tab | Fixed | The query is not literal, so CodeMirror reads `\n`, `\t`, `\\` in it and in the replacement; the seed only escaped real newlines and ignored regex mode | The seed doubles backslashes, or escapes pattern syntax in regex mode (the pane picks the right one); a plain replacement is passed with its backslashes kept. Typed `\n` in the query still finds a newline, as intended | `find.test.ts` "the term the bar opens on finds just what was selected" and "a typed query and replacement" (10 fail before) |
| EC37 | High | Words typed in a table cell and left by a tab switch (Ctrl+Tab, Cmd+W) within the 700 ms before the idle commit were lost, or written into the note switched to when it had the same table at the same place | Fixed | Switching calls `setState`, which tears the table down at once; its saved edit then landed in a microtask on whatever note the view held by then, and the cell's blur commit gave up because the cell was gone | The table takes `later(view)` with each keystroke and lands its kept edit through it, so a moved view writes to its own note; the outgoing note also flushes table edits before it is taken | `table/switching.test.ts` (4 tests; the first two and the last fail before) |
| EC38 | Medium | Words typed in a table cell and not yet written were wiped whenever another pane, a collaborator, sync or a reload changed the note above the table or another cell | Fixed | `adopt` compared the incoming table with the drawn one including the pending cell, always found a difference, and cleared the pending edit; with both place and text changed the widget was rebuilt and the edit dropped | The pending edit is carried into the incoming table when that cell is unchanged there (the other writer wins if it changed that very cell), and a moved table of the same shape and header is adopted, not rebuilt | `table/remote.test.ts` (4 tests; 3 fail before, the fourth pins the chosen rule) |
| EC39 | Medium | Regression from EC29: changing a tab-indented nested list's kind rewrote the tab as too few spaces, so the child left its parent; children not in the selection still fell out of a parent whose marker widened, and a child whose own parent was not selected could take another item's shift | Fixed | The marker-width change was applied to selected lines only, with the indent's character count used as its column | `toggleList` walks the document from the first selected line, carries each changed item's width to every line nested under it, measures columns with tab stops, and keeps tabs (it only adds spaces, or takes trailing ones away) | `commands.test.ts` "keeps tab-indented children nested…", "carries children that are not selected…", "leaves a child whose own parent did not change…" (the first two fail before) |
| EC40 | Low | Numbering a list that runs into a quote continued the outer numbers inside the quote (`> - a` / `- b` became `> 1. a` / `2. b`) | Fixed | Counts were kept by indent only | Counts and nesting are kept per quote | `commands.test.ts` "numbers each quote apart from the list around it" (fails before) |
| EC41 | Medium | Regression from EC38: words typed in a table cell could be written into a different table that took the first one's place (two templated tables, the first deleted by another pane within 700 ms); and a row added by another writer still wiped them | Fixed | The pending cell was matched by position and text alone, which an empty templated cell always passes; a changed row count cleared it | Each live table's start is tracked through the editor's changes (`tableTracking`), and words carry over only into the table whose start survived; a pending row that moved is found again by its contents | `table/remote.test.ts` "never puts its words into another table that took its place" (fails without the tracking) and "keeps its words and its row when another pane puts a row above it" |
| EC42 | Low | Every keystroke's Enter handling pulled the whole list-key module (9 KB) into what the app evaluates at start, pushing the desktop startup budget over | Fixed | EC21 put `continueLists` in `list-keys.ts`, which was loaded lazily before | `continueLists` lives with the other Enter commands in `commands.ts` | `apps/desktop/test/weight.test.ts` (see the budget note under open Lows) |
| EC43 | Medium | Enter on an empty `> ` line inside quoted code (a fence in a quote or callout) deleted the line and added no newline, cutting the block in two; a bare `>` line inside plain code or an HTML block written over several lines was eaten the same way | Fixed | `leaveQuote` runs first on Enter and matched the line's text against an empty quote without asking the syntax tree | `leaveQuote` steps aside inside code, HTML, comment and maths blocks | `leaving.test.ts` "a line that only looks like an empty quote" (2 tests, fail before, run through the real Enter chain) |
| EC44 | Medium | Undo and redo (keys, vim `u`, the input event) changed and saved a note in Read-only mode, including a note read-only because its space is shared to read | Fixed | A shared note undoes on its own internal state and carries the result to its panes as external, which the read-only filter lets through | `undoEdit`/`redoEdit` refuse on a read-only state | `shared.test.ts` "changes nothing in a note that is read-only" (fails before) |
| EC45 | Medium | Snippet completion opened on any word that began a trigger (`no` for `note`), also in code, and Enter, which accepts a completion first, expanded it: typing `I said no` and Enter made a callout | Fixed | Triggers were matched by prefix while typing, with no code check (an empty-match guard could never fire) | While typing, a snippet is offered only for its whole trigger; prefixes are offered on an explicit completion request; never in code | `snippets.test.ts` "does not offer an abbreviation that an ordinary word only begins while typing", "is never offered in code" (fail before) |
| EC46 | Medium | With smart punctuation on, quotes typed in a link title, an HTML tag's attributes or a `$…$` formula were curled before the construct was closed, breaking the link, the tag or the TeX | Fixed | The straight-quote check relies on syntax nodes that only exist once the construct is closed | `writingMarkup` also spares an open `](`, an open `<tag ` and an odd count of unescaped `$` before the caret on the line (a line with a lone price `$5` keeps straight quotes, an accepted trade-off) | `typography.test.ts` 3 tests under "markdown being typed" (fail before) plus one that prose after closed markup still curls |
| EC47 | Low | Regression from EC35: right after a note opened for editing (or a remote change), ArrowDown at a block drawn under the caret jumped over the block's source to its end | Fixed | The far-edge step applied whenever a block was drawn at the caret, not only when blocks never open | The far-edge step applies only where blocks never reveal (Read-only) | `stepping.test.ts` "never jumps over a block drawn under the caret of a note just opened for editing" (fails before) |
| EC48 | Low | A sub-pixel sideways wobble on a picture's resize corner still counted as a drag (EC31), and closing a fence could wait up to 200 ms on the parser in a very large note | Fixed | Any change in `clientX` started a drag; the forced parse had a long budget | A drag starts after 3 px; the fence parse waits at most 50 ms and otherwise leaves the fence as typed | `widget.test.ts` resize test extended with a 1 px move; fence tests pass |
| EC49 | Low | Clear formatting over a selection deleted every `*`, `==` and backtick outside code: `*` bullets lost their markers, `\*` kept a stray backslash, and `$a*b$` lost its operators | Fixed | Marks were stripped from the text by a regex, not read from the syntax tree | Only the emphasis, strikethrough, highlight and code-span marks the parser found are taken out | `commands.test.ts` "keeps star bullets, escaped stars and the stars of a formula" (fails before) |
| EC50 | Low | Insert table (Mod-Alt-T) with the caret in a table's lines did nothing, then focused that table's header cell with its words selected, so the next keystroke wrote over the header | Fixed | `insertTableToEdit` ignored that `insertTable` had refused and went on to focus the table at the caret | It stops when the table was not inserted | `table/insert.test.ts` (fails before) |
| EC51 | Low | A preview tab reused for another note while sync changes were held back during an IME composition threw `RangeError` on the next settle (or could misapply them) | Fixed | `takeOn` replaced the note's state but kept the held changes, which were relative to the old text | `takeOn` lets go of held changes, since the new text stands for the note | `shared.test.ts` "are let go of when the note takes on another note while they wait" (throws before) |
| EC52 | Medium | An answer streaming into an ` ```ai ` block was dropped while its tab was in the background: text that arrived meanwhile left a gap, nothing was written if the switch came before the answer started, and an answer that finished while away left the block showing a running ask | Fixed | The stream read and wrote `view.state`, which a tab switch swaps for the other note's state, where the run does not exist | The run keeps its own note: while the pane shows another note it catches up with the note's changes, writes through the note's `local`, and announces the run's effects, so the answer and its close land in its own note and never in the other one | `ai/run-switching.test.ts` (5 tests, fail before) |
| EC53 | Low | A task box ticked just before its pane moved to another note could tick a task with the same words in that other note | Fixed | After awaiting the task store, the retry read whatever note the view now held | The tick remembers its note and writes there when the pane has moved | `task-tick.test.ts` 3 tests (fail before) |
| EC54 | Low | A note dropped from the explorer just before the pane moved to another note was linked in that other note | Fixed | The link was written after an async import into whatever the view held | The drop takes `later(view)` and lands in its own note | `wikilink/drop.test.ts` (fails before) |
| EC55 | Medium | Typing in a link's hover preview card could undo what others wrote to that note while the card was open (a collaborator, an agent, sync): the card wrote "the note now, replaced by the card's copy" | Fixed | Each write took the note's current text as `before` and the card's stale copy as `after`, so the edit covered everything written meanwhile | The card writes from the text it last wrote (or opened on), so the workspace rebases only the card's own edit over the interim changes | `apps/desktop/src/lib/preview-card-base.test.ts` (fails before) |
| EC56 | Medium | A block dragged by its handle while the pane moved to another note (Ctrl+Tab with the button held) was cut and re-inserted inside the other note, or threw `RangeError`; the same stale span also misplaced a block when the note changed during the drag (an open Low before) | Fixed | The block's span was taken at the press and used on whatever state the view held at release | The drag lets go when the document is no longer the one it was pressed on | `block/carry.test.ts` (fails before) |
| EC57 | Low | Rewriting a picture (alt edit, resize, reset) could break it: a title holding `"` was written unescaped, an alt ending in `\` never closed, a whole `<img>` lost every attribute but src/alt/title/width (class, height, style), and `data-src`/`data-alt` were read as src/alt | Fixed | Markdown titles and alts were written with too little escaping, tags were rebuilt from scratch, and attributes were matched at a word boundary that `-` satisfies | Titles and alts escape and read back `\` and `"`; a tag is rewritten in place (`retag`), changing only src, alt, title, zoom and width; attributes are matched only as whole names | `image.test.ts` "a picture written again after an edit" (3 tests, fail before) |
| EC58 | High | Words typed in a table cell were dropped when another pane or a sync wrote the whole table again (for example after a column was widened elsewhere), a regression of EC41 | Fixed | The pending cell edit tracked the table start with `mapPos`, which returns nothing when the change covers the whole table, so the edit had no place to land | `track` falls back to finding the same header inside the inserted text, nearest the old offset, and marks the table as rewritten | `remote.test.ts` "keeps its words when another pane writes the whole table again" and "when the note is rewritten from above the table into it" (fail before) |
| EC59 | Medium | After such a rewrite the pending words could land in a different row that only shared the old cell text | Fixed | The pending place matched any row whose cell held the old text | Strict match at the same row first, then a unique strict match, and a loose same-row match only when the row count is unchanged and the other rows are untouched | `remote.test.ts` "never moves its words to a row above that only shares the cell it was typed in" (fails before) |
| EC60 | Medium | With reveal on, the arrow key next to a drawn block (table, maths) did nothing: the caret stood on the block edge and the key was swallowed, a regression of EC47 | Fixed | Stepping moved the caret to the edge it was already on, so nothing changed and the block never opened | When the edge is the caret itself, stepping asks the blocks to open there (`openBlocksHere`) | `stepping.test.ts` "opens that block on the press instead of pressing in place" (fails before) |
| EC61 | Low | Smart quotes stayed straight in prose after `a <b and it` or after a code span holding `$`, and a closing attribute quote checked the wrong text | Fixed | The tag test matched any `<word`, and the maths test counted `$` inside code spans and escapes | Tags count only while an attribute value is open (tested before the typed quote); maths ignores escapes and code spans | `typography.test.ts` new expectations (fail before) |

| EC62 | Medium | Making a list over an item holding a quote (`- a` then `  > note`) left the quote behind the widened item, so it fell out of the list, and the numbering restarted after a quote nested in an item (`- a`, `  > - x`, `- b` gave `1.`, `1.`, `1.`) | Fixed | Nesting compared whole quote prefixes, so a quote inside an item never counted the item as its parent, and any change of quote dropped every running count | Each line is read as its quote levels and the space before each `>`; parents and counts are matched level by level, the space before a nested `>` takes its item's shift, and only counts of levels that ended are dropped | `commands.test.ts` "carries a quote inside a list item along with its item" (fails before) |
| EC63 | Low | A snippet was offered while typing a front matter value | Fixed | Snippets only skipped code | Snippets also skip front matter | `snippets.test.ts` "is never offered in front matter" (fails before) |
| EC64 | Low | A language holding a backtick, typed for a backtick fence, turned the whole code block into prose | Fixed | The language field wrote any text after the fence; a backtick fence's info may not hold a backtick | Backticks are dropped from the language of a backtick fence; tilde fences keep them | `fields.test.ts` "never holds a backtick that would end a backtick fence" (fails before) |
| EC65 | High | A table cell's own write copied its words into another row that held the same words (two empty rows of a new table, or a row added with Enter), and the caret moved there, so further typing went into the wrong row (re-audit, regression of EC58/EC59) | Fixed | The table wrote itself while its pending cell edit was still held, so its own rewrite went through the "rewritten elsewhere" search, which found the twin row | The pending edit is let go before the table writes itself | `table/twin.test.ts` "a written cell is not copied into a row that held the same words" (fails before) |
| EC66 | High | Switching tabs while a code block's language field was open could throw, leaving the pane joined to no note: later typing reached neither note (re-audit, regression of EC64) | Fixed | The backtick check read the view's state when the field closed, which during a switch is already the other note | The check is made when the field opens, against the note it belongs to | `torn-down.test.ts` "a language typed for a code block survives a switch to a shorter note" (fails before) |
| EC67 | Medium | Enter after typing a fence (```` ```js ````) no longer closed it in the app (re-audit, regression of EC28) | Fixed | Orden's fence parser reads an unclosed fence as a paragraph, and the check wanted a FencedCode node; the tests used the plain parser | A fence line in prose (not in code, HTML, a comment, maths or front matter) counts as unclosed | `commands.test.ts` "pressing Enter after a code fence, as the app reads it" (5 tests, fail before) |
| EC68 | Medium | A preview tab reused for another note took pending writes meant for the old one: an AI answer still streaming, a slow task tick, a picture alt or a dropped link landed in the new note | Fixed | Writers only noticed a pane moving to another note object; a reused preview tab keeps the object and swaps its text (`takeOn`) | `SharedDoc` counts its turns; `later`, AI streaming and task ticks drop a write once the note has taken on another | `run-switching.test.ts`, `task-tick.test.ts`, `shared.test.ts` "tab is reused" tests (fail before) |
| EC69 | Medium | Making or taking off a list over a selection with blank lines filled them with markers (`- a`, blank, `- b` became `- a`, `- `, `- b`), so a loose list could not be taken off | Fixed | Picked blank lines were treated as list lines | Blank lines in a selection with words are left alone, and numbering runs on across them | `commands.test.ts` "leaves blank lines in the selection blank" (fails before) |
| EC70 | Low | One `$` on a line (a price) turned smart punctuation off for the rest of it (regression of EC61) | Fixed | Any odd count of `$` read as open maths | `$` opens maths only before a non-space, non-digit, and closes only after a non-space | `typography.test.ts` price expectations (fail before) |
| EC71 | Low | Rewriting an `<img>` (alt edit, resize, reset) dropped unquoted values, turned `width="50%"` into pixels, matched `width` inside another attribute's value, and reset or resize kept `height` (distorted); a markdown alt with an escape (`my\_shot`) gained a second backslash (regressions of EC57) | Fixed | Attributes were found by regex over the whole tag and rewritten whether changed or not; the alt writer escaped every backslash | Attributes are read as tokens, only changed ones are rewritten, height goes with a changed width, and a backslash is escaped only where it would read as an escape | `image.test.ts` 3 new tests (fail before) |
| EC72 | Low | After EC71: Reset size left the width on a zoomed `<img>` that also had one, a tag with an attribute written right after a quote (`alt="x"src="a.png"`, `<img/src=…>`) stopped being a picture or lost its zoom and gained a style on each resize, `width=300/>` lost its width, and Edit markdown put the caret short of the alt's end when the alt held `\\` (second re-audit) | Fixed | The width was left alone whenever the parsed width matched, even when the zoom changed; the attribute reader needed whitespace before each name; the width value kept the `/`; the caret was measured from the rewritten alt, not the source | The width goes when the zoom changes too; attributes may follow a quote or `/`; a trailing `/` is ignored; the caret goes to the alt's closing bracket in the source | `image.test.ts` 3 new tests (fail before) |
| EC73 | High | Words typed in a table cell jumped, with the caret, to a twin row (two empty rows of a new table) when another pane or the same view wrote another cell of the same row (third re-audit, regression of EC59) | Fixed | EC59 searched other rows for an exact match before accepting the same row with a changed neighbour | When the table was edited, not written again whole, the same row is accepted first | `table/twin.test.ts` "typed words stay in their row when another cell of that row is written meanwhile" (fails before) |
| EC74 | High | Every cell write after a pause redrew the whole table, dropping a selection inside the cell and cutting off an input method composition, so composed words could be lost (third re-audit, regression of EC65) | Fixed | EC65 let the pending edit go before the table's own write, so the incoming table no longer matched what was shown | While the table writes just its pending edit, the edit is kept in place and the cell is not drawn again; writes that change more (rows, sorting) or move focus still let it go first | `table/twin.test.ts` "a cell written after a pause is kept on the page, not drawn again" (fails before) |
| EC75 | High | Words typed in a table cell were dropped when one change covered the whole table and ran past it, as a sync or reload that edits above and below the table does (third re-audit, missed by EC58) | Fixed | Such a change replaces the table widget, and its last write went to where the table was before the change | The widget's last write goes to the tracked start of the table | `remote.test.ts` "keeps its words when the note is changed above and below the table at once" (fails before) |
| EC76 | Medium | Making a list over an item holding a quote with a blank line (`  >`) left that line behind, splitting the quote out of the item (third re-audit, missed by EC62 and EC69) | Fixed | Blank lines were skipped before their quote marks were moved | A blank line that holds quote marks still has the space before them moved with its item | `commands.test.ts` "carries a blank line of a quote inside an item along with the item" (fails before) |
| EC77 | High | Words typed in a table cell could still be lost or land in the wrong row while other writers touched the table within the 700 ms before they were written: a sync that reordered rows put them in another row (regression of EC73), a whole-table write from another pane moved them to a twin row, a sync that changed the note around the table and another cell dropped them, a renamed column dropped them, and the caret was lost after a change spanning the table (fourth re-audit) | Fixed, by design | A typed cell was held outside the note and matched back to a row by its words after every change; each round of matching rules (EC38, EC41, EC58, EC59, EC65, EC73-EC75) left other cases, because which row is "the same" cannot be told from the words alone | Cell edits are now written into the note as they are typed, as small per-line changes with the table kept aligned, so other writers' changes merge with them like any other text. What the focused cell shows is kept only while the note agrees with it, follows its words when their row moves, and the caret is put back after the table is drawn anew. The held edit, its row matching and its last-chance write are gone | `remote.test.ts` (12 tests, restated for words that are already in the note) and `twin.test.ts` 3 new tests (fail before) |
| EC78 | Medium | Taking an item off a list (the list button again) left its children behind: a child list or a second paragraph indented under the item turned into words of the item or into code (`- a`, `    - b` became the paragraph `a - b`) (fourth re-audit) | Fixed | Taking off only deleted the markers; only making a list moved the lines under each item | Taking off goes through the same pass as making a list, so the lines under an item move back by the width of its marker | `commands.test.ts` "takes an item off without taking its children or its later paragraphs out of the list" (fails before) |
| EC79 | Medium | Taking off the first item of a numbered list turned the rest of the list into paragraph text (`a`, `2. b`, `3. c` is one paragraph) (fourth re-audit) | Fixed | A list that starts at 2 cannot break a paragraph, and the items after were left numbered from 2 | The items after a first item taken off are numbered again from 1 | `commands.test.ts` "numbers the items after a first item that is taken off from one" (fails before) |
| EC80 | Medium | With several cursors, numbering ran on across an item that was not picked (`- a`, `- b`, `- c`, cursors on a and c, gave `1. a`, `- b`, `2. c`, and `2. c` joined item b) (fourth re-audit, older than this branch) | Fixed | Lines that were not picked never ended a running count | A line that is not picked ends the counts it is not inside | `commands.test.ts` "starts the numbers again after an item that was not picked" (fails before) |
| EC81 | Low | Reset size kept `height` on a zoomed `<img>`, so the picture stayed fixed at that height, and Edit markdown on an `<img>` could put the caret inside `data-alt` | Fixed | Height was only dropped when the parsed width changed; the caret used a regex | Height goes whenever the size changes; the caret uses the attribute reader | `image.test.ts` 2 new tests (fail before) |
| EC82 | Medium | Taking off a task (`- [ ] a` with lines under it) moved its children by the width of the box, so they ended up indented under nothing or read as code (fifth re-audit, regression from EC78) | Fixed | The shift of the children was measured from the task box, not from the list marker | The shift comes from the marker alone, and the box is taken off over the longer of the two | `commands.test.ts` "moves the children of a task taken off by its marker, not its box" (fails before) |
| EC83 | Medium | Taking off a numbered item in the middle of a list, when its children move out with it, left the items after it counting on from before (`1. a`, `3. c`), and a list of the other kind of numbers (`1)`) after it was numbered as if it were the same list (fifth re-audit) | Fixed | Only items taken off with nothing above them started a new count, and the count did not check the delimiter | Each numbered item taken off starts a count for what follows; it numbers only items of the same delimiter and stops at anything else | `commands.test.ts` "numbers the items again after a middle item whose children move out with it", "keeps the other kind of numbers apart" (fail before) |
| EC84 | Medium | Taking off a whole loose nested list left the nested items indented by four or more spaces, so they became a code block (fifth re-audit) | Fixed | The shift of lines that were themselves picked was set to zero | Picked lines keep the shift of the items they sit in | `commands.test.ts` "takes off a whole loose nested list without making code" (fails before) |
| EC85 | Medium | Making a list over items that hold code, a table or wrapped lines made each of those lines a list item of its own (fifth re-audit) | Fixed | Every non-blank line was listed | Lines that belong to an item's content (inside the item and indented to its content column, from the syntax tree) are carried with the item, not listed | `commands.test.ts` "leaves the code, tables and wrapped lines inside items alone" (fails before) |
| EC86 | High | With the caret in the note just above or below a table, typing in a cell showed the table as Markdown source after the first key (fifth re-audit, regression from EC77) | Fixed | Since cell words are written to the note on every key, the block layer read each write as typing near the caret and opened the block | Table writes carry a `tableWrite` annotation, and the block layer does not count them as typing | `twin.test.ts` "a table under the caret stays drawn while a cell is typed in" (fails before) |
| EC87 | High | After a remote edit, the caret in a cell could jump to another row that held the same cell words (fifth re-audit, regression from EC77) | Fixed | The caret followed the first cell with the same words, not its row | The caret follows its whole row: the same row if the row is unchanged, else the only matching row elsewhere, else the cell in the same row | `twin.test.ts` "the caret follows its whole row, not another row that shares the cell" (fails before) |
| EC88 | Medium | Typing in a cell of a large table was slow: about 29 ms a key at 3000 rows (fifth re-audit, from EC77) | Fixed | Every key wrote out and padded the whole table again | While the column keeps its width, only the edited row is written; the widths are kept from the last write. Measured in jsdom at 3000 rows: about 15 ms a key, of which about 10 ms is CodeMirror's own reparse of the table block, the same as for a plain edit there | Profiled with a temporary test (CPU profile); table suites pass |
| EC89 | Medium | The task list toggle (Mod-Shift-4) gave boxes to wrapped lines, code and blank lines inside the selection, so a task list could not be taken off again and code inside items broke (sixth re-audit) | Fixed | `toggleTaskList` had its own pass that changed every selected line | The task list now runs through the same pass as the bullet and numbered lists, which carries item content, skips blank lines and moves children by the marker | `commands.test.ts` "the task list carries wrapped lines and code with their item", "the task list leaves blank lines blank" (fail before) |
| EC90 | Medium | Mod-Enter put a box on a code fence or indented code inside a list item, and on the wrapped and blank lines of a selection; a line starting with inline code could not get a box (sixth re-audit) | Fixed | The code check looked at column 0, before an item's code starts, and counted inline code as code; every selected line was taken | The check looks for a code block at the line's own start, and a selection boxes its items, not their carried or blank lines | `commands.test.ts` "a box is not put on code inside an item", "a box goes on the items of a selection, not their wrapped or blank lines" (fail before) |
| EC91 | Medium | The quote toggle pulled lines out of list items: `> ` went in at column 0, and an indented `> ` was not seen as a quote (sixth re-audit) | Fixed | The prefix only matched `>` at column 0 and was always put at the start of the line | A quote is found after its indentation, and a new one goes in at the content column of the item the line belongs to | `commands.test.ts` "a quote inside a list item keeps to the item" (fails before) |
| EC92 | Medium | Making a list over a selection holding a code fence, table, maths block, HTML block or front matter outside any item put a marker on each of its lines, and turned `1. x` inside code into another marker (sixth re-audit) | Fixed | Only lines inside list items were kept out of the listing | Lines inside a block that holds its own lines are neither listed nor counted; a selection only of such lines does nothing | `commands.test.ts` "a list leaves code, tables and front matter outside items as they are" (fails before) |
| EC93 | Medium | Smart quotes and dashes were off on every line indented four or more spaces, such as third-level bullets and wrapped lines in items (sixth re-audit) | Fixed | Any line with four spaces in front was taken for indented code | Inside a list item the four-space rule is not used; real code there is still found in the syntax tree | `typography.test.ts` "and not the deeper lines of a list, which are indented but still prose" (fails before) |
| EC94 | Medium | Undo or redo in a cell hid the table and dropped the focus when the note's caret was on the table (a note that opens or ends with a table) (sixth re-audit) | Fixed | The undo from a cell is CodeMirror's own transaction, without the table-write mark, so the block layer took it for typing and opened the table | Commands run from a cell are marked as table writes through a transaction extender | `undo.test.ts` "keeps a table under the caret of the note drawn, and the cell focused" (fails before) |
| EC95 | Medium | Switching to another note with a table at the same place put the focus into that note's table, and the next key was typed there (sixth re-audit) | Fixed | The refocus after a table is drawn again did not check that the editor still showed the same note | A table remembers which showing of the editor drew it, and is not refocused after the editor was given another note | `switching.test.ts` "a switch to a note with a table in the same place leaves the focus out of that table" (fails before) |
| EC96 | Medium | An edit to another cell during an input method composition drew the table again and lost the composed words (sixth re-audit) | Fixed | Any outside edit redrew every cell, including the one being composed | While composing, an edit that keeps the table's shape updates only the other cells in place | `ime.test.ts` "keeps the words being composed when another cell is written meanwhile" (fails before) |
| EC97 | Medium | Sort by this column never sorted a column that was already in order the other way round; a refused column move shifted dragged widths while a cell held typed words (sixth re-audit) | Fixed | The order only flipped after a sort that changed something, and the unchanged check compared two fresh copies of the table | A sort that changes nothing tries the other order, and each command compares against one copy | `sort.test.ts` "sorting a column that is already in order puts it the other way round" (fails before) |
| EC98 | Medium | Quoting a whole list pulled code, tables and later paragraphs out of their items and split the list in two (seventh re-audit, regression from EC91) | Fixed | The quote went in line by line at each line's own item column, so the item lines got column 0 and their content got the item's column | The quote column is chosen once for each run of selected lines: the content column of the item that holds the whole run from above, else column 0 | `commands.test.ts` "quoting a whole list keeps every line in its item" (fails before) |
| EC99 | Medium | Quoting a nested item on its own moved it out of its parent (`- a` / `  - b` gave `>   - b`) (seventh re-audit) | Fixed | The item search stopped at the item that starts on the line itself | The search goes on to the item that holds the line from above | `commands.test.ts` "quoting a nested item keeps it under its parent" (fails before) |
| EC100 | Medium | A lazy line under a nested item (`- a`, `  - b`, `  c`) was listed as its own item, so the list could not be taken off (seventh re-audit) | Fixed | Item content was only checked against the innermost item, whose content column the line does not reach | The check goes up to the outer items until one carries the line | `commands.test.ts` "a lazy line under a nested item goes with the list" (fails before) |
| EC101 | Low | A list toggle on a blank line inside a code fence wrote a marker into the code, and a picture written as `<img>` on its own line could no longer be listed (seventh re-audit, from EC92) | Fixed | Blank lines skipped the block check; every HTML block was held | A selection of only blank lines inside blocks does nothing; HTML and comment blocks are held only when they span more than one line | `commands.test.ts` "a blank line in code and a picture on its own line" (fails before) |
| EC102 | High | Ctrl+Z or redo inside a cell scrolled the editor to the note's own caret, often far from the table, so the table being edited went out of view (seventh re-audit) | Fixed | Table writes never moved the note's caret, so the undo restored and scrolled to wherever it was before | Commands run from a cell keep the scroll position they started from (`scrollSnapshot`), and the note's caret is kept just beside the table while its cells are written | `undo.test.ts` "in a note / in a shared note, an undo in the cell keeps the view where it is" (fail before) |
| EC103 | Medium | Ctrl+Z in the note after typing in a table put the caret back at the old spot, which could be the top of the note or the table itself shown as source (seventh re-audit, same cause as EC102) | Fixed | As EC102: the history event started at the old caret | The caret is moved beside the table, outside history, before the first write, so the undo lands there | `undo.test.ts` "in a note / in a shared note, an undo in the note puts the caret beside the table" (fail before) |
| EC104 | Medium | An undo or redo in a cell that took the table away left the focus on the page body, so the next keys went nowhere (seventh re-audit) | Fixed | The refocus after a redraw only looked for a table at the old place | When no table is drawn there any more and the focus was lost, the note itself takes the focus | `undo.test.ts` "an undo that takes away a table just made gives the focus back to the note" (fails before) |
| EC105 | Medium | When another writer moved rows during an input method composition, the composed words were written over a different record (seventh re-audit, from EC96) | Fixed | The in-place update kept the focused cell although its row now held another record, and the end of the composition wrote the detached cell back | The in-place update is used only while the focused cell's own text is unchanged, and a detached cell never writes | `ime.test.ts` "does not write the words being composed into a row another writer moved there" (fails before) |
| EC106 | High | In a note opened at a remembered caret (every tab open, switch or restore), Ctrl+Z after typing straight away sent the caret to the top of the note and scrolled there (eighth re-audit) | Fixed | The shared note's own selection only followed caret moves the views reported; joining a note or setting its state never reported one, so the undo step started at a stale caret | Each edit reports the view's caret from just before it, and the shared note takes that caret before the edit is recorded | `undo-caret.test.ts` "an undo after typing at the caret a tab opened with goes back to that caret" (fails before) |
| EC107 | Medium | With two panes on one note, Ctrl+Z in one pane could put its caret at the other pane's place (eighth re-audit, same cause as EC106) | Fixed | As EC106 | As EC106 | `undo-caret.test.ts` "an undo in one pane goes back to its own caret, not the other pane's" (fails before) |
| EC108 | Medium | Quoting a list turned a quote inside an item (`- a` / `  > b`) into a lazy line of the item, so `b` was no longer quoted (eighth re-audit) | Fixed | A line already quoted only had its own `>` rewritten, never the new outer one | A line whose quote sits deeper than the run's column gets the new outer `> ` at that column | `commands.test.ts` "quoting a list nests a quote that is inside an item" (fails before) |
| EC109 | Medium | Two drops or pastes of files that overlapped while a file was still being written landed in the wrong places, the first drop at the second's point or at the caret (eighth re-audit) | Fixed | One drop point was kept for the whole editor; each drop replaced it and each landing cleared it | Each drop keeps its own mapped point, removed only when it lands | `images.test.ts` "each land where they were dropped, whichever is written first", "a paste meanwhile does not send the drop to the caret" (fail before) |
| EC110 | Medium | Ctrl+Z in a cell that undid an edit above the table (a paste, a deletion) threw the view off, up to the end of the note (eighth re-audit, from EC102) | Fixed | The kept scroll position was taken in the positions from before the undo and not mapped through its changes | The changes the command makes are collected and the kept scroll position is mapped through them | `undo.test.ts` "an undo in a cell of an edit above the table keeps the view on the table" (fails before) |
| EC111 | Low | The first key in a cell could reveal a diagram, maths block or heading right next to the table, because the note's caret was moved onto that line (eighth re-audit, from EC102) | Fixed | The caret was put on the line after or before the table whatever it held | The caret is put only on a blank line beside the table, and is left where it is when there is none; the refocus after a redraw skips an editor that is gone | `undo.test.ts` "typing in a cell leaves the caret off a heading right under the table" (fails before) |
| EC112 | Medium | Composed words could still go into another record when another writer moved rows that hold the same value in the focused column (status, yes/no) (ninth re-audit, from EC105) | Fixed | The in-place update only checked the focused cell's own text | It also checks that the focused row was not moved: a changed row whose old cells now stand in another row draws the table again | `ime.test.ts` "does not write the words being composed into a twin row another writer moved there" (fails before) |
| EC113 | Medium | Pasting paragraphs, a table or a fence into a task item indented the rest by the width of the box, so it read as an indented code block (ninth re-audit) | Fixed | The paste counted the `[ ] ` box as part of the list marker | Only the list marker sets the indent; the box is item content | `paste.test.ts` "line up under the words of the item, not under its box" (fails before) |
| EC114 | Medium | Quoting an item's paragraphs across a blank line put the blank line's `>` at column 0, which ended the item and pushed the later paragraphs out of the list (ninth re-audit) | Fixed | A blank line has no indentation to reach the run's column | A blank line is padded to the run's column before its `> ` | `commands.test.ts` "quoting the paragraphs of an item across a blank line keeps them in the item" (fails before) |
| EC115 | Medium | A file dragged in from the explorer landed at its old drop point after a slow read, mid-word if the note was typed in meanwhile, or in another note if the pane switched (ninth re-audit) | Fixed | The drop point and the note to write to were only taken once the read had finished | The drop is registered, mapped and tied to its note at once; the read is awaited inside | `images.test.ts` "lands where the drop point ended up after typing above it meanwhile" (fails before) |
| EC116 | High | Making an empty line a heading, quote, list or task left the caret in front of the new marker, so the next words went before `# ` or `- [ ] ` (tenth re-audit) | Fixed | The commands dispatched their changes without a selection, so the caret was mapped to the front of the insert | The caret is mapped past what is inserted at it in every block toggle | `commands.test.ts` "the caret on an empty line ends up after the marker put there" (fails before) |
| EC117 | High | The Run, Ask and Copy buttons did nothing for a fence inside a list item or a quote (tenth re-audit) | Fixed | The fence was looked up only at the line start, which sits before the item or quote marker, outside the fence | The fence is looked for at the position, beside it and at the start of the line's content; its container lead is read from its closing line | `fence.test.ts` "is found from the start of its first line, inside a list item or quote too" (fails before) |
| EC118 | Medium | An AI answer to a fence inside a list item or quote was streamed at column 0, which ended the item or quote and broke the note around it (tenth re-audit) | Fixed | The answer block and every streamed line were written without the fence's container lead | The fence's lead is applied to the answer's head, tail, gap and every streamed line; an existing answer is found with the lead stripped | `run.test.ts` "streams the answer inside the list item or quote the fence is in" (fails before) |
| EC119 | Medium | Pasting several paragraphs into a quote inside a list item lost the quote on the later lines (tenth re-audit) | Fixed | The container lead read only one level of item or quote | The lead takes every nested quote and item marker, with item markers turned into spaces | `paste.test.ts` "stay inside a quote that is inside an item" (fails before) |
| EC120 | Medium | Words in brackets such as `[sic]` or `[1]` lost their brackets and were styled as links that go nowhere; a callout marker was styled as a link; reference links `[text][ref]` did not follow their definition (tenth re-audit) | Fixed | Every Link node was treated as an inline link, though Markdown makes `[x]` a link only when a definition exists | Reference links look up their definition: an undefined label keeps its brackets, a defined one hides its label and links to the definition | `decorate.test.ts` "words in brackets with nothing defined for them keep their brackets", "a reference link shows its words and follows its definition", "the marker of a callout is not a link" (fail before) |
| EC121 | Medium | Arrow keys stepped the caret into a block hidden inside a folded section (tenth re-audit) | Fixed | The step aimed at the next block without looking at folds | A line inside a folded range is not stepped into | `stepping.test.ts` "not into a block that sits in a folded section" (fails before) |
| EC122 | Medium | Arrow down on a wrapped line jumped over the rest of the line into the block below (tenth re-audit) | Fixed | The step looked only at document lines, not at the rows a wrapped line is drawn on | The step is taken only when the caret cannot go down a row of its own line | `stepping.test.ts` "not while the caret can still go down a row of its own wrapped line" (fails before) |
| EC123 | Medium | With equation numbering on, `\eqref` and `\ref` showed stale numbers after an equation was added above, and two panes overwrote each other's labels (tenth re-audit) | Fixed | Labels lived in one map for every pane, and a maths widget compared only its source, so it was never drawn again when its number changed | Labels are counted per note state; a maths widget resolves its references when it is made and compares the resolved text | `equations.test.ts` "labels belong to their own note", "a reference redraws when the equation it points at is renumbered" (fail before) |
| EC124 | High | Asking an AI fence again after its answer's closing mark was lost deleted everything below, up to a later answer's closing mark or the end of the note: the writer's own notes and later questions (eleventh re-audit) | Fixed | The answer was taken to run to the next closing mark anywhere in the note, or to its end | An answer without its own closing mark, before any other answer opens, is not replaced; the new answer is written under the fence and the old words stay | `block.test.ts` "is nothing where the closing mark is gone, since what follows may be the writer's own", "is nothing where the next closing mark belongs to a later answer"; `run.test.ts` "keeps the notes and the second question written below it" (fail before) |
| EC125 | Medium | Bold, italic, strikethrough or code on a whole picked line put the marks around `# ` or `- [ ] `, which broke the heading or item (eleventh re-audit) | Fixed | On one line the selection was only trimmed of spaces; the heading, item and quote markers were skipped only across lines | The selection skips the line's block markers before the marks go on | `wrap-blocks.test.ts` "bold on a heading line bolds its words and keeps the heading" and two more (fail before) |
| EC126 | Medium | Highlight over several items or paragraphs, or over a whole picked line, wrapped the markers and the blank lines too, so the list or heading broke (eleventh re-audit) | Fixed | Highlight wrapped the raw selection, with neither the marker skipping nor the per-block split the other marks have | Highlight uses the same trimmed selection and, across blocks, marks each block's words on its own, with its colour | `wrap-blocks.test.ts` "highlight on a heading line keeps the heading and its line break", "goes inside each item, as bold does, and keeps the list", "goes on each paragraph, since a highlight ends with its block" (fail before) |
| EC127 | Medium | Heading up on a list item or task wrote `# - item`; Heading N over paragraphs made the blank lines between them empty headings (eleventh re-audit) | Fixed | Heading up skipped only quote marks, not the item marker; both commands changed every selected line, blank ones too | Heading up replaces the item marker as Heading N does; blank lines in a selection of several lines are left alone | `heading-commands.test.ts` (3 tests, fail before) |
| EC128 | Medium | Run, Copy and Ask did nothing for a fence written on the list item line itself (`- ```js`), and an answer to one was never written (eleventh re-audit, from EC117) | Fixed | The line's content start skipped quote marks but not a list marker, so the lookup landed on the marker | The content start skips list markers too | `fence-items.test.ts` (4 tests, fail before) |
| EC129 | Medium | Sort lines and Reverse lines in a list sorted the line introducing the list in with the items, moved children under other parents and kept old numbers (eleventh re-audit) | Fixed | The run was every non-blank line around the caret, sorted line by line | In a list, the items of the caret's list are sorted whole with their children, and a numbered list is renumbered | `lines.test.ts` "sorting and reversing the list the caret is in" (5 tests, fail before) |
| EC130 | Medium | Changing case rewrote link addresses, picture paths and block ids, which broke the links other notes make to that block (eleventh re-audit) | Fixed | The raw text of the selection was changed whole | Addresses, wikilink targets and block ids are kept; only the words around them change | `case.test.ts` "changing case over a note with links and block ids in it" (5 tests, fail before) |
| EC131 | Medium | Ctrl+V of paragraphs copied in Orden into a quote or list item put the later paragraphs outside it; Edit > Paste kept them inside (eleventh re-audit) | Fixed | Orden's own clipboard was handed to the plain paste, which adds no container lead | The keys go through the same placing as the menu | `paste-menu.test.ts` "both keep every paragraph inside the quote they are pasted into", "both keep every paragraph inside the list item they are pasted into" (fail before) |
| EC132 | Medium | A file dragged in from the explorer was linked by its bare name when another file had the same name, so the link opened the other file (eleventh re-audit, the explorer route of EC19) | Fixed | The explorer link used the name without looking for others of that name | One shared helper picks the space path when the name is shared, for drops of files and from the explorer | `note-links.test.ts` "a file is linked by its path so the link finds it and not the other file", "a picture embeds itself by its path and not the other picture of that name" (fail before) |
| EC133 | Medium | A link whose words held bold, code, an escape or a picture (`[the **best** docs](url)`, badges) was not drawn as a link and could not be followed; reference labels showed and bracketed marked words lost their brackets (eleventh re-audit) | Fixed | The closing bracket was taken to be the node right after the opening one, so any inline child made the link bail out | The link's brackets are its first two link marks, whatever sits between them | `link-words.test.ts` (7 tests, fail before) |
| EC134 | Medium | Find left a folded section shut when the match was its last word (eleventh re-audit) | Fixed | The editor opens a fold only when the caret head is strictly inside it, and a match ends where the fold ends | Find next, previous and replace open every fold the match overlaps | `find-fold.test.ts` "opens the fold when the word ends the section" (fails before) |
| EC135 | Medium | Home in a bullet or task item put the caret before the hidden marker, so typing wrote `x- item` (eleventh re-audit) | Fixed | The bullet and task markers are always hidden, and the line start sat before them | A move to the start of an item line from its words stops where the words start | `item-start.test.ts` (2 tests, fail before) |
| EC136 | Medium | A click at the edge of a `[[wikilink]]` put the caret inside its brackets, which opened it as source and typed into the link (eleventh re-audit) | Fixed | Wikilink brackets were not among the marks a click snaps out of | They are | `wikilink-snap.test.ts` (3 tests, fail before) |
| EC137 | Medium | `a == b and c == d` was drawn as a highlight in the editor, hiding the signs, though the reading view shows plain text (eleventh re-audit) | Fixed | The editor's highlight delimiter could always open and close | A `==` opens only before a non-space and closes only after one, as in the reading view | `spaced-equals.test.ts` "are shown in the editor too, not hidden as highlight marks" (fails before) |
| EC138 | Low | Smart quotes were curled in front matter typed from the top before its closing `---` (eleventh re-audit) | Fixed | Front matter is only parsed once it is closed | An open front matter block at the top keeps straight quotes | `typing-front-matter.test.ts` (fails before) |
| EC139 | Medium | Two note embeds on one line, or a picture beside a note embed, became one "Note not found" block that hid the line (eleventh re-audit) | Fixed | The line was read as one embed from its first `![[` to its last `]]` | A line is a block embed only when the brackets hold one link, as the inline parser reads it | `wikilink.test.ts` "beside another embed on its line stays a link, not one note named across both" (fails before) |
| EC140 | Medium | A note embed stayed stale when the line next to it changed: a blank line opened under it did not draw it, one taken out left it drawn (eleventh re-audit) | Fixed | Whether a line is an embed hangs on the lines beside it, but the local rebuild only rescanned what met the change | The local rebuild also rescans one line above and below each change | `blocks-nearby.test.ts` "a note embed whose neighbour line changes" (2 tests, fail before) |
| EC141 | Medium | A table made whole by typing words into its header stayed as source after the caret left (eleventh re-audit) | Fixed | Typing letters after letters was treated as plain prose, and a table not yet whole has no span to rebuild | An edit on or above a line with a `|` takes the local rebuild | `blocks.test.ts` "words typed into a header that make the table whole draw it once the caret leaves" (fails before) |
| EC142 | Medium | The table of contents showed `> ## Inside`, `- # Item heading` and hidden block names (eleventh re-audit) | Fixed | The whole document line was handed over as the heading's words | The heading node's own text is used, without its block id | `toc.test.ts` "the words of a heading in the table of contents" (3 tests, fail before) |
| EC143 | Low | `[toc]` with a long line of words right under it was not drawn (eleventh re-audit) | Fixed | The length cap measured the whole paragraph | The cap applies to the `[toc]` line | `blocks.test.ts` "a toc with a line of words right under it is drawn, however long the words" (fails before) |
| EC144 | Medium | Bold, italic, strike, code or highlight over several paragraphs or items of a quote, or over two headings one under the other, wrote one run across them, so the marks showed as text (twelfth re-audit, from EC125/EC126) | Fixed | Blocks were told apart only by a bare blank line or an unquoted list marker | A quoted blank line, a quoted marker and a heading line also part blocks | `marks-quoted.test.ts` (3 tests), `highlight-colour.test.ts` "goes on each paragraph" (fail before) |
| EC145 | Medium | Pasting an address over a whole picked line wrote `[- item](url)` or `[# Title](url)`, and a triple-clicked line pulled the next line up (twelfth re-audit) | Fixed | The paste wrapped the raw selection | It wraps the same trimmed selection the marks use, past the line's markers and break | `paste.test.ts` "over a whole line, keeps the item marker and the hashes outside the link", "over a line picked with its line break, keeps the break and the next line" (fail before) |
| EC146 | Medium | Insert link (Mod-K) over a whole picked line put the item marker or hashes inside the link (twelfth re-audit) | Fixed | The raw selection became the label | The trimmed selection becomes the label | `insert-link-lines.test.ts` (2 tests, fail before) |
| EC147 | Medium | Typing `*`, `_`, `~`, `=` or a backtick over a whole picked line or several blocks put the marks around the markers or across the blocks (twelfth re-audit) | Fixed | The typed mark went at the raw ends of the selection | One line uses the trimmed selection; several blocks get a run each, as with Mod-B | `typed-marks.test.ts` (3 tests, fail before) |
| EC148 | Medium | Turn into list or task from the block menu made every line of a paragraph its own item, and turned an item's second line or paragraph into a child (twelfth re-audit, the EC85/EC100 bug in a sibling command) | Fixed | Every line of the block got the new marker | Only a block's first line, or a line that starts an item, gets a marker; other lines move with their item's content column | `bulk.test.ts` "turning a block of several lines into a list" (3 tests, fail before) |
| EC149 | Medium | A coloured highlight started at a caret lost its colour on the first key (twelfth re-audit) | Fixed | The colour prefix was selected, so typing replaced it | At a caret the caret goes after the colour prefix | `highlight-colour.test.ts` "keeps its colour when the words are typed in" (fails before) |
| EC150 | Medium | Home in a numbered task, Home on a heading or quote line with quiet marks on, a click on a bullet and ArrowUp or ArrowDown onto an item line put the caret before the hidden marker (twelfth re-audit, gaps in EC135) | Fixed | Only bullets, and only moves from the words of the same line, were corrected | Any caret that lands in the hidden marker run of a line (bullet, task box, numbered task, and heading or quote marks when quiet) moves to where the words start; ArrowLeft from the words goes to the line above | `item-start-lines.test.ts` (5 tests, fail before) |
| EC151 | Medium | Ctrl+D, Select all occurrences and Select every match put carets inside a folded section and left it shut, so the next keys edited hidden text (twelfth re-audit, gap in EC134) | Fixed | Only Find next, previous and replace opened folds | Every command that selects matches opens the folds its ranges overlap | `find-fold-carets.test.ts` (3 tests, fail before) |
| EC152 | Medium | Jumping back from a footnote to a mark that ends a folded section left the fold shut with the caret hidden (twelfth re-audit) | Fixed | The jump set the caret at the fold's end, where the editor does not open it | The jump opens the folds that hide its target | `footnote-fold.test.ts` "opens the fold when the mark ends the section" (fails before) |
